**Last reviewed (prior):** 2026-07-23/24 (Window B1/B2 — ruin geometry + survivable-edge frontier, isolated worktree `session/20260723T233912Z-b-ruin`) — B1: registered **GG-135** (renumbered from a session-local GG-134 that collided with a concurrent session's own, independently-registered GG-134 — the `mc_empirical_bootstrap.py` engine coverage-gaps entry, merged first at `6b21f52`): MAIN book's real per-bet variance (σ=1.081%/bet log-return) × real cadence (15.83 bets/day) trips the permanent 20% drawdown kill switch near-100%/yr **even at zero edge** — the risk config self-terminates before any edge can prove out, independent of whether Window A's calibration fix lands. B2: swept `scripts/mc_survivable_edge_sweep.py` (new, wraps `mc_empirical_bootstrap.py`) for (a) minimum per-bet edge to hold 1yr P(kill)≤10% at today's real sizing/cadence — **MAIN +0.169%/bet** (~27x its best-ever observed cohort mean of +0.006%/bet); **T4 ≈ −0.0005%/bet** (already near-survivable at zero edge — T4 is edge-limited, not ruin-limited) — and (b) the Kelly-fraction/cadence/DD-width frontier by assumed edge. Key structural finding: Kelly-fraction cuts reduce variance **quadratically** (stake scaling), cadence cuts only **linearly** — at zero edge, cutting MAIN's Kelly to 10% survives (P(kill)=1.2%) but cutting cadence to 10% does not (P(kill)=79%), nor does widening the DD switch to 50% (P(kill)=71.5%). **Implication for a future Window B3 ADR: if MAIN's real edge doesn't clear ~+0.17%/bet, Kelly-fraction reduction is the only lever with enough leverage — cadence throttling and DD-width widening are both order-of-magnitude too weak alone.** Hit and resolved a live GG-ID collision mid-session-end (rebase conflicts in `GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md`/`DECISION_LOG.md`, both correctly non-`merge=union`) via the established renumber-the-unmerged-side-with-provenance-note convention (GG-113/GG-129→130/GG-131→132 precedent) — no destructive action, other session's entry untouched. ## T-388 — Bump-time behavioral-activation check for logic-epoch boundaries — ✅ DONE, 2026-07-21

GG-124's process gap had **two** motivating cases (the GG-113/119/123 boundary-stacking blackout; T-387's data-dependency miss). Built the proposed check as `scripts/check_epoch_activation.py` (Python, not the originally-sketched `.sh` — reused the existing `cron_log_aggregator.py` SSH-embedded-script pattern for the remote half):

1. **`check_stacking()`** — warns (never blocks) if more than one `logic_epoch.py` boundary landed within a trailing 7-day window (GG-124's own "one week" re-check horizon, reused as the threshold). Takes injectable `boundaries`/`now` so it's unit-testable without mutating real dated history.
2. **`check_field_activation(field, artifact_glob)`** — SSHes to the VPS, finds the most recently modified file matching the glob, and confirms the named field is present/non-null in at least one record. Zero-present-with-nonzero-total is flagged as the exact GG-105/T-387 failure signature (deploy landed, artifact didn't carry the new field yet). Handles no-files-matched, unreachable host, nonzero remote exit, and malformed output as explicit ALERTs.
3. `logic_epoch.py` gained a public `get_boundaries()` accessor (script reads it rather than reaching into the private `_BOUNDARIES` tuple) and a new docstring section pointing at the check.
4. Exit code semantics: stacking is warn-only and never affects exit code (per the original "warn, not block" requirement); field-activation ALERTs return 1.
5. `tests/test_check_epoch_activation.py` — 17 cases (stacking window edges, field present/entirely-null/partially-null/no-files/nonzero-exit/timeout/malformed-output, CLI exit-code paths) via injected boundaries and mocked `subprocess.run`. Full suite: 2751 passed, 28 skipped, 0 failed.

Not wired into a pre-commit hook or the bump workflow itself — it's a standalone script to run manually (`scripts/check_epoch_activation.py [--field X --artifact-glob Y] [--skip-vps]`) before/after committing a boundary, matching the "checklist/script" framing this task was registered under. Automatic invocation from the bump workflow, if wanted, is a future task.

See DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-21 (T-388 row), `scripts/check_epoch_activation.py`, `src/prediction_market_bot/logic_epoch.py`.

## T-376 — GG-091 mypy remediation: lowest-risk category fixed + verified (369→203 errors) — PARTIAL, real multi-session progress, GG-091 remains OPEN, 2026-07-16

**Isolated worktree `20260716T053339Z-gg091-mypy-remediation`.** Explicit multi-session scope, not a one-shot close-out: re-ran `mypy --strict` fresh to confirm the true current baseline (369 errors/58 files, drifted from a previously-logged 365/57), categorized by risk/effort, and fixed only the lowest-risk tier — `type-arg`+`no-untyped-def`+`var-annotated`+`unused-ignore` = 174 errors across 42 files outside the 4 reserved files (`c4_risk_engine/*`, `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`, `paper_trader.py`, `logic_epoch.py` — all correctly untouched). 156 `type-arg` sites fixed via a line-targeted regex script; 18 remaining `no-untyped-def`/`var-annotated`/`unused-ignore` fixed by hand.

**Self-inflicted incident, caught and fixed before verification completed:** a supporting "ensure `Any` is importable" script (`/tmp/ensure_any_import.py`) had a real bug — it scanned each file for the last import-like line with no awareness of indentation context or string/docstring boundaries. This corrupted 4 files: `run_exp010.py`, `run_pregame.py`, `run_paper_trade.py` each got `from typing import Any` inserted mid-body at column 0 inside an indented `try:`/`if:` block, breaking Python's syntax outright (mypy fails closed on any syntax error tree-wide, so this blocked the entire verification run); `run_exp001.py` got it inserted inside a docstring — syntactically harmless but semantically inert, producing 3 `[name-defined]` errors on `Any` instead. Found via a full `ast.parse()` sweep of every file in `src/` (independent of mypy's own fail-closed behavior) plus the subsequent full mypy run surfacing the docstring case. All 4 fixed by moving the import to the top-level import block and removing the stray line; re-verified via `ast.parse()` (0 syntax errors tree-wide) and a full mypy re-run (0 `[name-defined]` errors).

**Final verification:** `mypy --strict src` completes a full, uninterrupted run at **203 errors in 46 files** (down from 369/58 baseline — 166 fewer errors, 12 fewer files). `pytest -q` full suite: **2722 passed, 28 skipped, 1 warning** — identical to the pre-fix baseline, zero regressions. Zero leakage into the 4 reserved files confirmed.

**Remaining OPEN backlog (next session's target, ~203 errors):** `import-untyped`≈88, `arg-type`≈24, `call-overload`≈21, `no-any-return`≈20, `union-attr`≈19 (mostly dark/frozen NBA `run_t4_live.py`), `assignment`≈15, `attr-defined`≈4, `misc`≈2, `return-value`≈1, `operator`≈1. GG-091 status updated with a superseding "Status (update 3)" entry (a stale mid-session "update 2" entry claiming zero fixes is left in place for the historical record, not deleted, per Hard Rule 10). GG-091 remains OPEN — this is genuine incremental progress, not a close-out. See `DECISION_LOG.md` 2026-07-16 (T-376 row), `GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md` GG-091.

## T-384 — GG-105 H2: blend pregame `p_t1` into the `ba_l10` shrinkage prior — OPEN, registered 2026-07-17 (T-382)

Explicitly deferred from T-382/ADR-0047's scope, not dropped: H2 proposes using the frozen C3 pregame model's `p_t1` (rather than, or blended with, the flat league-average `PRIOR=0.2512`) as the shrinkage target for low-AB players, on the theory that pregame calibration already carries player-specific signal the flat league prior discards. **GG-069 (OPEN, MEDIUM, no task number assigned yet) — `fetch_actual_hits`/`_fetch_mlb_hits` (`run_resolve_mlb.py`) has no FINAL-game-status gate.** It queries the MLB Stats API `gameLog` endpoint and returns whatever hit total is present for the matching `game_date`, with no check of `abstractGameState`/`codedGameState`. `gameLog` updates live mid-game, so in-progress games can and do resolve with partial hit totals. ## NEW TASKS — T4 MLB Throughput Diagnosis (set 2026-07-02, GG-076)

**Context:** Earl asked for a root-cause diagnosis of T4 MLB firing only ~10-20 bets/DAY against CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9's documented design of ~1,000 evaluations/game -> 50-100 fired bets/game. Diagnosed via direct analysis of real production data (`exp_output/MLB/snapshots/t4_mlb_cycle_log_SNAP-2026-07-02-002.jsonl`, 2,393 cycle-evaluations, 2026-07-02) plus source inspection of `run_t4_mlb_live.py`, `run_mlb_pregame.py` (PIPE-06pre), and `c4_risk_engine/risk.py`. Full finding registered as **GG-076**. Earl's ruling: CLAUDE.md's documented throughput target is authoritative; the implementation is what needs to change. "At optimum" per Earl = each bucket firing with correct math/filtering/decision trees/execution paths — not a P/L claim.

| # | Task | Owner | Priority | Notes |
|---|------|-------|----------|-------|
| T-225 | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-07-03.** Reconcile T4 MLB one-shot-per-ticker dedup (`already_fired_game` in `run_t4_mlb_live.py::_run_cycle`) against CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9 | DS (design decision) -> EL (implementation) | HIGH | **Earl's design ruling: "allow re-entry per ticker."** The permanent one-shot dedup was correct scaffolding while the Binomial math, contamination guards, kill switch timing, and C4 edge gate were unproven — it is not needed now that those are proven (kill switch drilled at 0.34ms, contamination guard verified, edge math confirmed responsive to real Kalshi price moves). The real backstops against over-concentration are `max_single_event_exposure_usd`, the drawdown kill switch, and the T4 band budget cap, not a blunt never-re-enter proxy. Implemented: `already_fired_game` changed from `set[str]` to `dict[str, tuple[int,int]]` mapping ticker → `(h_so_far, ab_remaining)` at last entry; a ticker is blocked from re-firing only when the current cycle's `(h_so_far, ab_remaining)` exactly matches its last-fired state, so re-entry is gated on the live state that actually feeds `p_t4` having moved. `PaperTrader.open_position()` gained optional `h_so_far_at_entry`/`ab_remaining_at_entry` kwargs (default `None`, backward-compatible with all non-T4 callers) so the guard survives a process restart; `_reconstruct_fired_tickers()` renamed `_reconstruct_fired_ticker_states()`, now scans `open_positions + closed_positions` sorted by `entered_at` so the most recent entry per ticker wins regardless of open/closed status. Verified no downstream code assumes ticker uniqueness: `run_resolve_mlb.py::_resolve_pool()` and `PaperTrader.settle_position()` both operate on individual `position_id`-keyed records, so multiple same-ticker positions resolve independently and correctly. Full rationale in DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-03 (3 entries: ruling, implementation design). 11 new tests added (`TestReconstructFiredTickerStates` x6, `TestRunCycle` re-entry x3, `TestOpenPosition` entry-state x2); full suite 2293 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (feature CSV staleness) — no regressions. See GG-076 (updated), T-229 (now unblocked, DS-owned). |

PIPE-06pre's full-day `pregame_mlb_20260702.json` shows `markets_fetched=234`, `players_matched=51`, spanning **6 of 9 games** (PIT@PHI, CIN@MIL, MIA@COL, STL@ATL, TB@KC, SD@LAD) — not 3. The "63 tickers/3 games" figure that seeded GG-076 came from the T4 *live cycle log* (`t4_mlb_cycle_log_SNAP-2026-07-02-002.jsonl`), which only captured a ~69-minute window (19:09-20:18 UTC / 15:09-16:18 ET, per T-227) — exactly when only the day's 3 earliest-starting games (12:35/14:10/15:10 ET) were in progress; STL@ATL (19:15 ET), TB@KC (19:40 ET), and SD@LAD (22:10 ET) hadn't started yet inside that window. This is expected behavior of a live-in-progress evaluator sampled over a short window, not dropped coverage. Genuine (smaller) venue gap found instead: 3/9 games that day (CWS@CLE, DET@TEX, LAA@SEA) had **zero** KXMLBHIT markets at all — real Kalshi coverage ceiling ≈ 60-70% of the day's games, not ~33%. Cross-day check (7 days, 2026-06-26 to 07-02): `markets_fetched` ranged 138-306, `players_matched` 31-74 per day — consistent with broad coverage, not truncation. Kalshi client pagination (`kalshi_client.py:483-503`) correctly loops via cursor until a short page — no bug found. **Verdict: (a) not a fixable ingestion gap — pagination is correct; (b) not a ~3-game venue hard-cap — real cap is ~6-7 games/day; (c) the low daily fired-bet count is NOT primarily a Kalshi-coverage problem — weight shifts back onto T-225's dedup policy as the dominant lever.** See DECISION_LOG.md entry 2026-07-02 and GG-076 update. |

| T-230 | Fix silent skip-on-None in `_reconstruct_fired_ticker_states` for pre-T-225 positions | EL | **MEDIUM (status re-checked 2026-07-04 CONTINUATION — count/date-range corrected, exposure window now confirmed PERMANENTLY CLOSED for the historical positions found; fix remains deferred per Earl's standing "leave queued" choice)** | Found during independent post-deploy diff review (2026-07-03, confidence-gradient check, not caught by prior T-225/226/227 work): reconstruction filters out any position missing `h_so_far_at_entry`/`ab_remaining_at_entry` (i.e. any position opened before the T-225 deploy) entirely, rather than treating "state unknown" as a defensive re-fire block — `if ticker and h_so_far is not None and ab_remaining is not None: states[ticker] = (...)` silently drops the ticker instead of recording a conservative sentinel. Net effect: if a process restarts mid-game while an open position exists from before this field existed, `already_fired_game.get(ticker)` returns `None` (not found), and `None == (ls.h_so_far, ab_remaining)` is never True, so the guard does not block a same-cycle re-fire on a ticker that may already hold an open position. **Correction (2026-07-03, T-231 session): the "no known live exposure" claim above was wrong** — found 10 positions, all `game_date=2026-07-03`. **Second correction (2026-07-04 CONTINUATION, item-2 status re-check): the T-231 session's "10 positions, 07-03-only" figure was itself an undercount.** Direct query against the live `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` on the VPS (all `open_positions` + `closed_positions` with `h_so_far_at_entry=None` or `ab_remaining_at_entry=None`) found **35 positions**, spanning **three game dates — 2026-07-01, 2026-07-02, and 2026-07-03** — not one. The T-231 session's check evidently only looked at the most recent date. Cross-checked all 11 open positions dated 2026-07-04 (the current live date at time of this re-check, confirmed via `date -u` and `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl`'s most recent entries): all 11 have clean, populated `h_so_far_at_entry`/`ab_remaining_at_entry` — the T-225 fix is confirmed working correctly for every position opened since its real restart. **Why this is nonetheless still safe to leave queued, not urgent:** Kalshi KXMLBHIT tickers encode the specific game date in the string itself (e.g. `KXMLBHIT-26JUL02...`), so a ticker from a 07-01/07-02/07-03 game can never be polled or re-evaluated by any live cycle again — those game-dates are permanently in the past relative to any future T4 cycle. This means the 35-position gap is not merely "dormant today," it is **structurally unable to ever produce a live duplicate-re-entry event**, for these specific 35 positions. The underlying code defect (silent None-skip instead of a blocking sentinel) remains real and would recur for any *future* schema migration to this dict — that risk is what stays open, not these 35 positions specifically. **Side finding (new, out of T-230's original scope, surfaced while re-verifying it — registered separately, see GG-079/T-234, not fixed here):** 3 of the 07-01 positions in this same gap carry `kalshi_order_status: "failed: HTTP 410"` (order never executed — market gone by submission time) yet remain counted in `open_positions` rather than a rejected/failed bucket. Fix for the original T-230 defect unchanged from prior sessions: on missing fields, insert a sentinel that can never equal a real `(h_so_far, ab_remaining)` pair (e.g. `(-1, -1)`) so an unknown-state ticker defaults to blocked, not open. Still deferred per Earl's explicit "leave queued" choice — this re-check confirms that choice remains safe (zero live exposure now or forecastably), it does not change the recommendation to fix opportunistically rather than urgently.

That's fine") — kept the one substantive caveat (Kalshi KXMLBHIT depth ceiling, GG-045) as a single plain paragraph instead of a repeated warning banner. **v3 addendum, same day — operator flagged scope gap: "Why is the MC still not scripted properly to include the full bot ... Where is T-4? ~1000s of bets PER GAME... is still not being simulated?"** Confirms the T1-only scoping decision in the original pass (justified at the time by T4's thin 13-bet sample) was the wrong call to make unilaterally. T4's true win rate still cannot be pinned from 13 resolved bets (a single flipped outcome swings it 7.7pp), so rather than guess one number, ran two explicit labeled scenarios: **Scenario A (breakeven, 46.34% win rate — the rate at which T1's own payout structure nets to exactly zero, which is what T4's real +$1.59-on-13-bets is statistically consistent with)** and **Scenario B (53.06%, T1-comparable, explicitly unproven for T4 — T4M-ARITH/FLOW remain formally BLOCKED pending their own experiments).** Finding: **Scenario A — 29% of paths killed on day 1, 96.5% by day 5, ~100% by day 15, frozen at ~$884 (-11.6%) for the rest of the year** (a driftless/zero-edge random walk at this bet frequency hits a fixed drawdown barrier almost immediately — not a bug, a property of high-frequency zero-edge processes under a hard stop). **Scenario B — 15.4% breach, plateauing by day ~15 (even faster than T1's day-150 plateau, same $500-cap-escape mechanism, compressed by ~13x higher frequency), median bankroll $8,851,815 at day 365** — an even less credible number than the T1-only figure, decisively confirming that including T4 does not shrink the "ludicrous" long-horizon problem, it amplifies it in whichever direction the true edge points. Combined (T1+T4, paired by path index) day-30 capital: ≈$4,534 under Scenario A, ≈$603,988 under Scenario B — Scenario A is the more defensible current-state reading given T4M-ARITH/FLOW's BLOCKED status. **Conclusion, now evidenced by both T1 and T4 legs independently: the fix is a shorter trustworthy planning horizon (30-60 days), not which legs/constants get tuned — recommended explicitly in the new artifact rather than left as an open question.** Methodology note (disclosed in-artifact): T4's 341 bets/day required a CLT day-chunking approximation (10 chunks/day × ~34 pooled bets/chunk via Normal approximation) rather than bet-by-bet simulation, to fit compute budget — a known conservative bias on kill-switch detection (may miss within-chunk dip-and-recover events), meaning true T4 breach probabilities are very slightly higher than reported, not lower. Also surfaced, not resolved: a data-provenance discrepancy between the MEMORY_BANK S116 T1 bankroll figure ($1,075.88, 3 open) and a same-day EXP-017 snapshot file (`paper_portfolio_mlb_20260703.json`, $926.12, 14 open) — flagged for awareness, not reconciled this session. New artifact `t1-t4-mlb-mc-combined` supersedes `t1-mlb-mc-v2-spaghetti`.

(2) `EXP-FLOW-MLB-01.md` additionally claimed `t4_price_cache.py` was the live MLB Kalshi price source; it is not — that module is Phase-8-BLOCKED NBA-oriented infra, never imported by `run_t4_mlb_live.py`'s live loop, which instead populates `live_prices: dict` directly from `pm.mid_price`/`pm.vig`. Both docs corrected to cite the real source, `logs/t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl`, once built (see next).

**Production fix (T-219 continuation):** added unconditional per-cycle raw-context logging to `run_t4_mlb_live.py` — every live evaluation cycle now appends a JSONL record (including a `game_date` field, added mid-session after a schema-gap review) to `logs/t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl`. This is what makes the GG-067 doc corrections true rather than aspirational — the log now actually exists in the live code path.

**Backtest runner built:** `scripts/run_exp_arith_mlb_01.py` (36 tests, 36/36 passing) grid-sweeps T4M-ARITH's `entry_window`/`confidence_gate` constants against `logs/t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl`, resolving real outcomes via `fetch_actual_hits` (MLB Stats API) through a dependency-injected resolver (`build_resolution_cache(cycles, resolver)`) so CI makes zero live network calls. Key design insight: because `T4MlbArithEvaluator` reuses the exact same frozen binomial `p_hits_gte_threshold(...)` function with the exact same inputs as the already-live T4M-PROJ evaluation, a cycle-log record's `p_t4` field is mathematically identical to `p_arith` for that record — no separate arithmetic re-derivation needed in the backtest. Applies ADR-0024 commercial validation tiers (Tier 1 n≥150, Tier 2 n≥500) and the T-177/GG-037 edge-monotonicity guard (Fisher's exact test → MONOTONIC/ANTI-PREDICTIVE/INCONCLUSIVE) to whatever data accumulates. Chronological (no-shuffle) splitting per project-wide temporal-integrity policy. Kalshi taker fee (`0.07 × mip × (1-mip)`) applied in P&L.

**Result as of this session's close: BLOCKED, not run — by design, not by omission.** The cycle log had zero records at deploy time because the logging feature only went live with this session's own deploy, and no MLB game was in progress at the 16:34 UTC deploy moment. `T4M-ARITH` correctly remains `status="BLOCKED"` in the ADR-0039 sub-band table — infra/code authorization is not the same thing as data existence, which is not the same thing as capital authorization. Confirmed safe restart window first (all games `STATUS_SCHEDULED` at restart time).
- **T-218 registered, ADR-0039 drafted** (`03_DECISIONS/ADR-0039_mlb-native-t4-subband-architecture-scoping.md`). Read `run_t4_mlb_live.py` directly first (applying GG-061's process fix prospectively — verify target code's actual domain before authorizing changes) — confirmed it's a single flat Binomial evaluator (`KELLY_T4=0.10`, one 45s ESPN-poll loop), the same "monolithic T4" problem ADR-0029 fixed for NBA, never inherited by MLB because MLB T4 was built independently (T-190/T-203/T-204).
- ADR-0039 does **not** copy the NBA roster verbatim. Four structural differences drove an MLB-native taxonomy: (1) MLB C1 is poll-only (45s ESPN diff), not event-push; (2) MLB's core signal (a resolved at-bat) is a deterministic state transition, not a probabilistic nudge like an NBA field goal; (3) pitcher identity/platoon split is an MLB-unique info class; (4) PA frequency is slot-gated/sparse (~3.1-4.2/game via `_AB_BY_SLOT`), organized around innings not a continuous clock.
- 10-sub-band Tier B roster proposed (T4M-PROJ reclassifying the existing flat model as the architectural spine, plus EVENT/PITCHER/LINEUP/FLOW/BOUNDARY/ARITH/STAGED/CORR/EXIT), all EXP-required per Principle 5 — no numerics proposed. Tier A (three-layer architecture, Principles 1-6) adopted unchanged from ADR-0029 as sport-agnostic; ≤5s kill-switch SLA explicitly flagged as NOT inherited (was derived from NBA's continuous per-second event density) — provisionally retains the existing file-based `t4_mlb_kill_switch.flag` (halts within one 45s cycle) pending re-derivation.
- **GG-066 registered** (OPEN): MLB C1 has no real-time/sub-poll-interval event feed — blocks T4M-EVENT, T4M-LINEUP, and real-time T4M-ARITH firing. Candidate resolution: MLB Stats API live game feed v1.1. T4M-BOUNDARY and T4M-ARITH flagged as cheapest next build (poll-driven, no new data source required). Also flagged but not registered as its own gap: `_AB_BY_SLOT` is 9-inning-only and understates `ab_remaining` in extra innings — low-effort fix candidate independent of this ADR.
- **No code authorized.** T-218 is scoping-only; each sub-band requires its own data-prerequisite resolution, EXP, and Phase 8 Activation Gate before implementation.

| **T-187H — t=3 shadow gate re-evaluation (GG-042 resolution)** | ✅ COMPLETE — **VALID NEGATIVE — structural finding confirmed.** GG-042 resolution condition required re-running T-187B with t=3 after T-187G (live SP overlay) completed. Re-evaluation run on full 3-season feature matrix (`exp_output/MLB/mlb_feature_matrix.csv`, 126,421 rows, holdout cutoff 2026-01-01). SP coverage prerequisite verified: 99.9% of t=3 events (h≥3) have `opp_sp_is_known=1` in training data — the GG-042 data quality premise was DISPROVED. Shadow gate outcome: t=3 Brier delta=+0.0002 (FAIL — same as T-187B), ECE=0.0037 (PASS), AUC=0.6141. Score distribution: p50=0.037 (≈ 3.9% base rate); only 487/25,021 holdout predictions exceed 0.08 (Kalshi market price = 1.9%). Root cause is structural: at 3.9% base rate, even AUC=0.61 cannot produce absolute Brier delta ≥0.001 — this is an information-theoretic ceiling, not a data quality problem. **No code changes.** `THRESHOLDS = [1, 2]` and `_EXCLUDED_THRESHOLDS = [3]` in `train_mlb_model.py` unchanged; `_MODEL_THRESHOLDS = frozenset({1, 2})` in `run_mlb_paper_trade.py` unchanged. Tests: 1790/1790 (unchanged). GG-042 status updated to DEFERRED — structural. DECISION_LOG entry appended. |

Root cause: opp_adj_lambda uses regular-season defensive ratings; Finals defensive intensity suppresses scoring 9-30% beyond what the model accounts for. (5) This is a feature engineering gap, NOT a model class failure. Poisson architecture is valid; the λ input is wrong. |

| Bloomberg terminal rewrite | **`scripts/live_terminal.py` — multi-panel curses layout replacing global-scroll single-pad approach** | ✅ COMPLETE 2026-06-03 SESSION 27 — Full rewrite: 8 independent panels (PORTFOLIO, LIVE POSITIONS, MARKET SNAPSHOT, TODAY'S BETS, ALERTS, RECENTLY RESOLVED, SYSTEM STATUS, T4 LOG TAIL), each with its own curses pad and `scroll_y`. 2-column layout (56%/44%). Layout adapts to terminal size via `_compute_layout()`. Atomic curses update via `doupdate()` — zero flicker. |
| Curses ordering bug fix | **Fixed blank-panel rendering: stdscr erased content areas; pad writes were invisible** | ✅ COMPLETE 2026-06-03 SESSION 27 — Root cause: `draw_panel()` called `ps.pad.noutrefresh()` inside each panel draw, then `stdscr.noutrefresh()` ran last → stdscr's blank content areas overwrote pad content in curses' virtual screen buffer → `doupdate()` rendered blank panels despite data present. Fix: moved all pad `noutrefresh` calls to a separate `refresh_pad()` pass that runs AFTER `stdscr.noutrefresh()`. stdscr wins on borders; pads win on content. Data confirmed flowing: 1416 T4 positions, $499.95 deployed, 3 T1 bets. |

## SESSION 2026-06-02 SESSION 13 — OPEN TASKS (dark period / Phase 8 pre-activation)

| # | Task | Owner | Priority | Notes |
|---|------|-------|----------|-------|
| T-128 | **DS-02 ADR: require live rolling pace as mandatory feed field** | DS | **HIGH — Phase 8 pre-activation** | KI-024: `pace_pts_per_min` is set at T1 from C2 feature store and never refreshed during a game. λ = pace × time_remaining drives both T4 and T5 Poisson CDF — a stale pace propagates linearly into every in-game probability estimate. At T5 (≤3 min remaining), λ is small and pace error translates directly to P(hit threshold) error. The Phase 8 DS-02 ADR must explicitly require live rolling pace as a required feed field, not optional enrichment. Without this constraint, a DS-02 source that provides only score + clock (no pace) would silently degrade T4/T5 inference. Resolution: operator reviews DS-02 ADR draft and adds live pace as a required field before signing. |
| EXP-018 | **T4_MIN_DELTA_P calibration experiment** | DS | **MEDIUM — Phase 8 pre-activation** | GG-007 (item 1): T4_MIN_DELTA_P=0.08 retained for paper prototype to generate calibration data. Requires replay of historical NBA game-state snapshots. Gate: must complete before Phase 8 T4 activation ADR is signed. |
| EXP-019 | **T5 certainty threshold calibration experiment** | DS | **MEDIUM — Phase 8 pre-activation** | GG-007 (item 2): T5_YES_CERTAINTY=0.85 / T5_NO_CERTAINTY=0.15 grounded by Poisson arithmetic (SESSION 14). Requires calibration against historical Q4 ≤3 min game-state data. Gate: must complete before Phase 8 T5 activation ADR is signed. |

## Standing Items (Monthly — GG-074 process fix, adopted 2026-07-06)

- [x] **Grounding-docs staleness review — first cadence run 2026-07-23.** Re-ran a file-by-file staleness audit across the same 28 files flagged with a "Last reviewed" date of 2026-05-21 through 2026-05-29 (~2 months stale): all 6 of `00_ADMIN/`'s, all 4 of `01_SPEC/`'s, all 8 of `02_ARCHITECTURE_DESIGN/`'s, `07_TEST_VALIDATION_EVIDENCE/TEST_STRATEGY.md`, and all 9 of `08_MODEL_RISK_GOVERNANCE/`'s. Each file was read in full and cross-checked against current `CLAUDE.md`/`MEMORY_BANK.md`/`GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md`/`DECISION_LOG.md` state (not just date-bumped). Result: 20 files confirmed current with no drift (header refreshed with verification trail); 8 files had genuine drift found and fixed in place (notably: `ASSUMPTIONS_LIMITATIONS.md`'s and `MODEL_CARD.md`'s own prior 2026-07-06 correction notes had themselves gone stale — GG-004's n-count sign-flipped since that note was written, and the v2-065b9510 "production model" framing conflated trained-vs-deployed; `PERFORMANCE_METRICS.md` was missing EXP-004 Part B's actual completion; `VALIDATION_SIGNOFF.md` had G5 marked IN PROGRESS when it closed same-day 2026-05-28). One new gap registered: **GG-133** (`CHANGELOG.md` unmaintained since 2026-05-30, ~8 weeks of major changes missing). This confirms the process fix works as designed — this is exactly the failure mode GG-074 exists to catch, caught on the very first run of the cadence it created. **This is now a proven recurring process, not a one-off remediation pass — re-run monthly per the rationale below, next due ~2026-08-23.**
- [ ] Rationale (unchanged): the structured, point-in-time documents that describe the system's designed shape, as distinct from the rolling logs (MEMORY_BANK.md, this file, GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md, DECISION_LOG.md) that already get updated every session, decay silently because each document's own "staleness trigger" (e.g., "gate passed," "ADR accepted") fires without prompting a re-read of the document that named the trigger. The rolling-log discipline (Hard Rule 7, session-start Step 1) does not cover this failure mode — it needs its own standing trigger, independent of any single gate closing. Tie next run to the same cadence as MEMORY_BANK.md's Compass OS Health M1 reporting.
